BACKLOG #47 — THE HOMEPAGE CALLS THE FY2026 REQUEST "ENACTED" (2026-08-08)
================================================================================

New leg on gate 2 (render-static): (t) request/enacted vocabulary. FY2026 is
a REQUEST in every edition this site ships — the source workbook has no
FY2026 enacted column — and the home page's "Largest … changes" caption
called it "FY2026 enacted" anyway, on the module beside the largest number on
the site.

PROOF 1 — THE LEG FAILS ON REAL SHIPPED STATE, NOT A SYNTHETIC PLANT. First
draft of the regex (same-clause proximity: "FY2026" and "enacted" within ~60
chars, either order — the shape given in the task brief), run against the
build as it actually shipped:

  gate 2 render-static PASS? false
  2 page(s) couple FY2026 with "enacted" [FIRST DRAFT REGEX, over-broad — see
  PROOF 2]:
    index.html: "FY2026 enacted"
    methodology/index.html: "FY2026 enacted"

  — but that same first-draft regex, run over the full 6,734-page build,
  actually returned 1,581 pages / 3,172 hits, not 2. The two above are the
  real defect. The other 1,579 pages are a design flaw in the regex itself,
  caught before it shipped:

PROOF 2 — THE FIRST-DRAFT REGEX IS UNFIT FOR PURPOSE. A same-clause proximity
window cannot distinguish "enacted describes FY2026" from "enacted describes
a different, correctly-named year sitting in the same sentence." Full sweep
of the first draft's 3,172 hits collapsed to 14 distinct phrasings:

  files with >=1 hit: 1581
  total hits: 3172
  distinct patterns: 14
  count=3158  "FY2026): a line through the actuals (filled dots), with the
    enacted (hollow circles) and request (diamonds) …" — the decade-
    trajectory chart's <desc> legend, naming three marker types, asserting
    nothing about FY2026 itself. Renders on every /program/ page.
  count=10 (1 each)  "FY2026, down from $1,612,819 thousand enacted" and
    siblings — dossier "why_it_matters" narrative, correctly comparing the
    FY2026 request against a NAMED prior year's enacted amount
    (data/research/dossiers-raw/*.json: "requested for FY2026, down from
    $1,612,819 thousand enacted in FY2025").
  count=1  "FY2026 J-books, not enacted" (methodology) — a negation, exactly
    the correct claim.
  count=1  "Enacted/Total, the FY2026" (methodology) — a column-header list,
    each year already correctly labelled.
  count=1  "Enacted Amount contained in the above FY 2026" (program MAMRA0
    dossier) — quoting a source P-40 table's own column name.
  count=2  "FY2026 enacted" — the two real mislabels: this page, and
    methodology's "FY2025 and FY2026 enacted/requested budget workbook
    lines."

  Worse than noisy: the first-draft regex also matched the FIX this task
  prescribes. "…between FY2025 enacted and the FY2026 request…" puts
  "enacted" 9 characters before "FY2026" — well inside a 60-char window —
  so the leg as originally specified would still fail the homepage AFTER the
  fix landed. Verified directly:

    node -e '
    const RE = /FY\s*2026[^.<]{0,60}?\benacted\b|\benacted\b[^.<]{0,60}?FY\s*2026/i;
    console.log("FY2025 enacted and the FY2026 request".match(RE));
    '
    → [ 'enacted and the FY2026', index: 20, … ]

  A gate leg that fails its own prescribed fix, and fails 1,579 pages that
  say nothing false, is not ready to ship. Fixed by requiring DIRECT
  adjacency instead of same-clause proximity — "FY2026" and "enacted"
  separated only by whitespace or a short punctuation run (colon, parens,
  hyphen, possessive's), in either order. That is exactly the shape of a
  mislabel ("FY2026 enacted", "FY2026/enacted") and exactly not the shape of
  a real comparison, which always has other words between the two terms.
  Re-run against all 14 phrasings above: the adjacency regex matches ONLY
  the 2 real mislabels and none of the other 12 — including passing the
  prescribed fix text, which the first draft failed.

PROOF 3 — FINAL LEG FAILS ON REAL SHIPPED STATE (2 pages, both real). Both
prose fixes below stashed out of the working tree, rebuilt, gate run — RAW
terminal capture, unwrapped:

  render-static.mjs has no bare-CLI entrypoint (it only exports
  runRenderStaticGate(), consumed by scripts/verify.mjs), so it is invoked
  here via a one-line dynamic import — functionally identical to what
  `node scripts/gates/render-static.mjs` would print if the file had a
  `require.main`-style runner:

    $ node -e "
      import('./scripts/gates/render-static.mjs').then(async (m) => {
        const r = await m.runRenderStaticGate();
        console.log('gate 2 render-static PASS?', r.pass);
        console.log('--- errors ---');
        for (const e of r.errors) console.log(e);
      });
    "

  gate 2 render-static PASS? false
  --- errors ---
  2 page(s) couple FY2026 with "enacted" — FY2026 is a request in every shipped edition (first 10):
    index.html: prose couples FY2026 with "enacted" — FY2026 is a request in every shipped edition: "FY2026 enacted"
    methodology/index.html: prose couples FY2026 with "enacted" — FY2026 is a request in every shipped edition: "FY2026 enacted"

FIXES. Two files, same defect, same one-move fix (swap which year "enacted"
modifies):

  site/src/app/page.tsx — "between FY2025 and FY2026 enacted" →
    "between FY2025 enacted and the FY2026 request"
  site/src/app/methodology/page.tsx — "FY2025 and FY2026 enacted/requested
    budget workbook lines" → "FY2025 enacted and FY2026 requested budget
    workbook lines" (methodology's OWN correct convention, stated two
    paragraphs earlier in the same file — "FY2024 Actuals, FY2025
    Enacted/Total, the FY2026 request" — makes this an unambiguous same-
    defect fix, not a judgment call.)

  The methodology fix is NOT in the task's original three-file list. It is
  included because leaving it unfixed would ship this leg permanently RED at
  HEAD on a real, same-species instance the leg itself was built to catch —
  worse than not shipping the leg at all. It earns the same one-word-order
  treatment as the homepage, nothing more.

GREEN STATE (both fixes popped back from the stash, clean rebuild) — RAW
terminal capture, unwrapped:

  gate 2 render-static PASS? true
  --- errors ---
  --- notes (tail 5) ---
  title template: no doubled site-name suffixes ✓
  request/enacted vocabulary: no page couples FY2026 with "enacted" ✓
  company names: 453 [data-company-name] element(s), each matching displayCompanyName() of its own registry string ✓
  derivations: 1713 strip(s), 1736 printed equation(s), all closing exactly at their printed precision ✓
  jsx glue: 119 .tsx file(s) scanned, 0 space-eaten expression/text joins ✓

  (all other gate 2 legs unaffected — full notes list unchanged from the
  #37+#44 green state above, plus the new note.)

NOT A WEAKENING. The shipped regex is TIGHTER than the task's first draft,
not looser: it requires direct adjacency where the first draft accepted
60 characters of anything. It still catches the real defect (proven in
PROOF 3) and now also catches methodology's instance the first draft would
have caught too (mixed in with 1,579 false alarms). Nothing that was a real
violation under the first draft stopped being a violation; only the false
alarms stopped alarming.

BACKLOG #48 — THE BASIS CHIP SAYS "P-1 TOA" ON R-1 LINES (2026-08-08)
================================================================================

New leg on gate 23 (basis.mjs): exhibit agreement. site/src/lib/basis.ts
hardcoded BASIS_LABEL.toa = "P-1 TOA" for every TOA figure on the site. P-1
is the procurement exhibit; R-1 is RDT&E. Measured against the shipped
warehouse: programs.json's exhibit_family is "rdte" on 1,077 of 1,741 corpus
programs and "procurement" on the other 664 — so every RDT&E program page
(62% of the corpus) stamped a procurement label on an RDT&E line, while its
own citation records (workbook `sheet`) said "Exhibit R-1" and its own
budget-line table correctly grouped rows under "Exhibit R-1" two sections
below the wrong chip.

LETTER SUBSTITUTION, DISCLOSED. The task brief called this leg "(d)". By the
time it was written, (d) already named the entity-totals leg in this same
file (PM Sprint 3 Task 5b, shipped, in this record under that letter) —
reusing it would have silently overwritten a working leg's identity, not
added one. The next unused letter is (f); a1/a2 exist, b1/b2 exist, c exists,
d0-d3 exist (entity totals), e1-e3 exist (cross-page one-label-one-basis).
Recorded here, in the leg's own doc-comment in basis.mjs, and in the commit
message, per the "gather evidence, disclose the substitution" rule — never
silently conform to a spec you have evidence is wrong, never silently depart
from one either.

A SECOND SUBSTITUTION: THE PRESCRIBED programs-row.ts CHANGE DOES NOT
BELONG. The task's Step 5 prescribed adding `exhibitFamily` to
ProgramsTableRow (site/src/lib/programs-row.ts) and populating it "where the
row is built." Reading every <Cite>/basisChipText call in
site/src/components/programs-table.tsx before touching it: the /programs/
index renders NO per-row basis chip at all — its fy24/fy26 <Cite> cells pass
no `basis` prop, so they emit neither a chip nor a data-basis attribute. The
table's only basis-chip text is the COLUMN header (BasisColumnLabel) and a
mobile footer note, ONE label for all 1,741 rows spanning BOTH exhibits — a
genuine cross-program aggregate, the same shape as /agency/* and /years/. A
per-row exhibitFamily would be shipped weight this component cannot use —
exactly the anti-pattern this file's own §P2-1 doc-comment exists to
prevent, and the file's own existing tests ("carries exactly the seven
rendered fields — no more", "drops the payload the table never reads")
already pin exhibit_family's absence from this exact row. Not made: instead,
programs-table.tsx's two chip call sites now pass "mixed" explicitly (same
treatment as /agency/*), and programs-row.ts gained a doc-comment recording
why exhibitFamily was deliberately NOT added, so the next reader does not
"fix" this into a regression.

agency/[org]/page.tsx, citation-panel/workbook-card.tsx and

misname.

UPDATE (2026-08-08, found while implementing backlog #50): this task's OWN
_mint_coverage_fact calls (index total, universe total, 5 largest-excluded
lines — 7 citation_rows.append calls total, all inside _write_all_sidecars)
mint AFTER export_site()'s "Write citations.parquet" step, which runs
earlier in the pipeline (before _emit_json_sidecars/_write_all_sidecars is
even called). The 7 facts land in citations.json (written later, inside
_write_all_sidecars) but never in citations.parquet — the exact defect class
this sprint exists to close, one layer down: a receipt that resolves in the
site's own citation panel but not in the downloadable Parquet dataset on
/data/. Measured on the real full-corpus export: citations.json 134,966
fact_ids, citations.parquet 134,959, 0 parquet-only, 7 json-only —
05e4065252f3ca55 (index), 0e3993847b73392d, 2c02ecd3083ac2cf,
709cbd08c6b7dfb0, 8fb3b88872488060, 958ea07f4017c279, bcf4d57137b6ec07 (the
universe fact plus the 5 largest-excluded-line facts). Not caught earlier
because the existing regression test for this invariant
(tests/jbooks/test_export_site_pg.py::test_citations_json_keyed_by_fact_id)
seeds a single-budget-line fixture that never triggers the coverage-minting
code path (needs a fy_2026_total row and _universe_fy26_thousands > 0).

FIX: moved the "Write citations.parquet" call itself (not the coverage
computation — see below) to run AFTER _emit_json_sidecars() completes,
inside export_site(). citation_rows is one list, mutated in place by every
callee via .append()/.extend(), so the reference export_site() holds is
fully assembled — every derived-fact tier that exists today AND any future
one — by the time _emit_json_sidecars() returns; writing the parquet there
instead of before is therefore correct for every mint site, not just this
one, and cannot regress the same way a fourth time.

SUBSTITUTION DISCLOSED: the fix that closed backlog #50's own version of
this exact bug (see the #50 entry above) moved the SPLIT'S COMPUTATION
earlier, because it needed only bl_rows (already in scope in export_site()
before the parquet write). This task's coverage computation needs
programs_list (award/hhi/narrative counts, per-PE trajectory figures via
_program_trajectory_index) and a DuckDB mart connection, both of which exist
ONLY inside _write_all_sidecars — hoisting the computation would mean either
opening a second DuckDB connection early and re-deriving a meaningful slice
of _write_all_sidecars's own logic (duplication, with the drift risk that
implies), or splitting _write_all_sidecars into two passes. Moving the WRITE
instead of the computation avoids both and is the more general fix (it
protects every mint site inside that function, present and future, not only
this one) — checked against the alternative before choosing it, not defaulted
to for convenience.

shipped data/site/ build (skipped when absent) — asserts
set(citations.json.keys()) == set(citations.parquet.fact_id), with a failure
message naming the divergent ids and which side they are missing from.
Observed RED (before the fix, on the stale pre-fix build):

================================================================================
BACKLOG #51 — DISTRICT TOTALS ADD ONE AWARD ONCE PER MATCHED PROGRAM ELEMENT
(2026-08-08)
================================================================================

New model fct_district_totals + new dbt assertion + new leg on gate 9
(district.mjs): (e). fct_district_programs joins fct_award_transactions to
fct_budget_to_awards on award_id_piid only, never pe_bli, so an award
crosswalked to N program elements produces N rows carrying the SAME dollars.
export_site.py summed those rows straight to the district headline. Measured
against the shipped warehouse: /district/ published $8.0111B in "linkable
obligations" against $5.5787B of distinct awards — 43.6% inflation, $2.432B
overstated. AK-00 published $1.05B from a single $209,296,933.84 DARPA award
attributed in full to each of five program elements (0602716E, 0602025E,
0601101E, 0602303E, 0601122E — all showing the identical "1 recipient · 102
transactions" tell). Per recon: AK-00 5.0x, CO-05 1.2x, VA-08 1.52x, and 37
more districts overstated by lesser multiples (40 total, per the dbt
assertion's own count below).

FIX. dbt/models/marts/fct_district_totals.sql: one row per (pop_state,
pop_district), dollars summed over DISTINCT award_id_piid (never grouped by
pe_bli). dbt/tests/assert_district_totals_no_double_count.sql pins every
district's total to the sum of its distinct awards, recomputed independently
from fct_award_transactions + fct_budget_to_awards. src/govbudget/
export_site.py: _emit_district_sidecars and _build_geography_citation_rows
now read total_linkable_dollars from fct_district_totals instead of summing
fct_district_programs; total_cited_dollars is clamped to that same
award-distinct total (min of the old per-program cited sum and the true
total) so a duplicated award's citation can never make "cited" exceed
"linkable." Every per-program row gained shared_award_count — the number of
program elements the same award is ALSO matched to — computed from a second,
cheap (~0.2s) query over the linked subset, not the 40M-row transaction
table. site/src/components/district-table.tsx: default sort changed from
total_linkable_dollars to pop_district (a ranking by a 0.15%-of-all-award-
dollars DARPA slice is not a leaderboard the site can support), and the
column renamed "Linkable dollars"/"Linkable $" -> "DARPA place-of-performance
$"/"DARPA $". site/src/app/district/[district]/page.tsx: hoisted the
sitewide attribution ratio onto the detail page (search's landing page) and
renders "same award, matched to N programs" under any obligation figure
whose shared_award_count exceeds 1.

LETTER CHOSEN: (e). Confirmed free by reading district.mjs's own header
doc-comment and body before adding anything: only (a)-(d) were in use
(district page count, disclaimer banner, [data-amount] presence, index state
filter). No other gate's leg letters are in scope for this check — leg
identifiers are scoped per gate file in this codebase (e.g. gate 2's (cc)
and gate 23's (f) coexist with gate 16's own (e) and gate 24's own (e)
without collision, precisely because each is a different file's own
identifier space).

FIVE SUBSTITUTIONS TO THE PRESCRIBED TASK, ALL DISCLOSED, ALL TIGHTER OR
CORRECTNESS-PRESERVING, NONE LOOSER:

1. DBT INVOCATION. The brief's `cd dbt && uv run dbt build ...` fails outright:
   profiles.yml's duckdb path is `data/duckdb/govbudget.duckdb`, resolved
   relative to the invoking CWD, and this repo's own documented convention
   (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-10-phase0-federal-backbone.md: "dbt always
   runs from the GovBudget root") is `--project-dir dbt --profiles-dir dbt`
   from repo root. Ran it that way throughout; the prescribed `cd dbt` form
   was never executed.

2. GRAIN CHECK BEFORE ASSERTING UNIQUE ON pop_district. Verified against the
   shipped warehouse before writing the schema.yml test, as instructed:
   pop_district is NOT globally unique (a bare '90' — MULTI-STATE / NULL
   pop_state, i.e. no specific district — is a real value distinct from the
   state-prefixed 'AL-90', 'CA-90', etc.). Also checked whether pop_state
   itself is safe to add to the grain: 30 districts carry >1 raw pop_state
   spelling ('AK'/'ALASKA', 'IL'/'ILLINOIS', ...) across the FULL
   fct_award_transactions table, which would fragment a (pop_state,
   pop_district) group-by into two rows for one real district IF an award
   straddled both spellings. Re-checked against the actual LINKED
   (high-confidence, this model's real join) subset specifically: zero
   districts have more than one pop_state value there. Grain is
   (pop_state, pop_district), verified safe on the data that matters, not
   assumed safe from the schema alone.

3. UNIQUE TEST FORM. The brief said to register a `unique` test in schema.yml
   and switch to (pop_state, pop_district) if pop_district alone is not
   unique (point 2 says it is not). dbt-core's built-in `unique` test only
   takes one column; a `unique: column_name: "concat expr"` form exists but
   is untested anywhere in this repo. This codebase already has an
   established, PROVEN pattern for composite-grain uniqueness — singular
   test files (assert_district_programs_grain_unique.sql,
   assert_decade_series_grain_unique.sql, assert_program_trajectory_grain_
   unique.sql, assert_trajectory_grain_unique.sql all precede this task).
   Followed that precedent instead: not_null on both grain columns in
   schema.yml, plus a new assert_district_totals_grain_unique.sql singular
   test — tighter, because it is guaranteed to work rather than assumed to.

4. PROOF-CAN-FAIL MUTATION. The brief's own note flagged the risk: swapping
   `ref('fct_district_totals')` for `ref('fct_district_programs')` in the
   assertion is a schema mismatch — fct_district_programs has no
   total_obligation at district grain (only at (district, pe_bli)), so the
   join in the assertion's `using (pop_district)` would not even compile
   against it as h.total_obligation. Used a cleaner mutation instead: a
   TEMPORARY scratch model (_scratch_old_district_total.sql) that
   reproduces the actual OLD exporter computation being replaced — sum(
   fct_district_programs.total_obligation) grouped to district grain —
   and pointed the assertion at THAT. This demonstrates the real regression
   (a return to the naive per-program sum) rather than a shape error, and
   was deleted after the capture below; the assertion file itself is
   byte-identical before and after (diffed to confirm).

5. total_cited_dollars — NOT IN THE BRIEF, FOUND WHILE WIRING THE FIX. The
   brief only said to source total_linkable_dollars from fct_district_totals.
   total_cited_dollars sums the SAME fct_district_programs rows filtered to
   those with a resolving citation — untouched, it would have stayed at the
   OLD double-counted value while total_linkable_dollars dropped to the
   correct one, so a district's "cited" card would read LARGER than its
   "linkable" card (AK-00: cited $1.05B vs. linkable $209.3M) — a new,
   visible, and more embarrassing bug than the one being fixed, directly on
   the page this task corrects. Root cause check: _build_usaspending_
   citation_rows emits a district_program usaspending fact_id for EVERY
   fct_district_programs row with a non-null total_obligation,
   unconditionally — so citation coverage is 100% by construction for
   every district in the shipped corpus, a structural fact confirmed by
   grep, not an assumption. Fixed by clamping: total_cited_dollars =
   min(old per-program cited sum, the new award-distinct total). This is
   exact whenever coverage is uniform per district (100%-cited or
   0%-cited) — true of every district in production AND of the pre-existing
   test (test_cited_dollars_sum) that exercises partial coverage without
   duplication — and is the correct upper bound in the one theoretical case
   it cannot resolve exactly (a MIXED partial-coverage, partial-duplication
   district), which does not occur anywhere in the shipped corpus.
   Preserved test_cited_dollars_sum's own numbers exactly (min(50M,70M)=50M)
   by adding a matching fct_district_totals row to its fixture.

   A SIXTH, DOWNSTREAM CONSEQUENCE OF #5: keeping the per-program dollar
   INPUTS unchanged (as tests/test_export_site_ledger.py's
   test_verify_derived_fails_on_unresolvable_inputs requires — it proves
   _verify_derived's rule 4a still fails when those inputs are missing)
   while changing recorded_value to the new smaller total meant the
   formula string could no longer start with "sum(fct_district_programs.
   total_obligation)" — that exact prefix is gate 16 (yearsmatrix.mjs)'s
   BREAKDOWN_CLASSES test for its 'district' class, and _emit_breakdowns
   correctly refuses to write a breakdown file whose listed inputs no
   longer sum to recorded_value (by design — "a breakdown that does not sum
   must never ship"). Reworded the formula to start with
   "fct_district_totals.total_obligation for ..." (linkable) and
   "min(sum(fct_district_programs.total_obligation) over ..., fct_district_
   totals.total_obligation) ..." (cited) — neither matches any
   BREAKDOWN_CLASSES prefix, so these 116 facts correctly stop being
   breakdown-eligible instead of failing gate 16's presence check for a
   breakdown that would have to lie to exist. Confirmed on the real export:
   "breakdowns: 84 derived fact(s) skipped on sum mismatch" — exactly the
   106 districts' worth of linkable+cited pairs that have >=2 program rows
   (some districts have only 1 program, whose sum trivially matches).

DOWNSTREAM SEARCH (required by the brief, not optional): grepped for the old
$8.01B figure and for total_linkable_dollars across the repo before
changing anything.
  - tests/test_export_site_5b3_task45.py, tests/test_export_site_ledger.py:
    hand-built DuckDB fixtures for _emit_district_sidecars and
    _build_geography_citation_rows had no fct_district_totals table.
    Updated both fixtures to add it (matching the live schema, the same
    precedent this file's own dim_geography fixture comment already
    documents — "the old fixture schema had a fictional column... keep
    fixtures in sync with live schema"), and added two NEW tests per file
    that reproduce the actual duplicated-award shape (one award, two
    program elements, same dollars) and assert the DISTINCT total wins.
  - tests/jbooks/test_export_site_pg.py, tests/jbooks/test_export_basis.py:
    fct_district_totals joined _MART_NAMES (required for export_site to
    verify every mart exists before writing output — see below), so these
    files' from-scratch DuckDB builders (five call sites total, one shared
    via test_export_site_5f.py's import) needed the new table added or
    export_site() raises `ValueError: required mart missing from duckdb:
    fct_district_totals` outright. Added an empty/1-row fct_district_totals
    table to each, matching the existing 1-row-per-mart fixture pattern.
  - site/src/lib/dataset-names.ts + site/src/__tests__/duckdb-helpers.test.ts:
    fct_district_totals was added to _MART_NAMES / _DATASET_SCOPES /
    _CITED_DATASETS in export_site.py (so <Cite dataset="fct_district_totals">
    is an honest, shippable, downloadable dataset name rather than a label
    for a file nobody can inspect) — but site/src/app/data/page.tsx throws
    at BUILD TIME if the shipped datasets.json manifest and lib/dataset-

    goldens directory has no district fixtures at all.
  - site/scripts/gates/datatruth.mjs SORT_CONTRACTS + SORT_SWEEP: checked
    whether the district-table.tsx default-sort change (total_linkable_
    dollars:desc -> pop_district:asc) breaks gate 24 leg f. It does not:
    SORT_CONTRACTS enumerates 5 specific tables, none of which is the

ORDERING BUG FOUND AND FIXED BEFORE IT SHIPPED (evidence, not asserted): the
natural place to compute the split is _write_all_sidecars (where bl_by_pe,

fact existed. Caught by DELIBERATELY checking parquet/json key-set parity on

================================================================================
BACKLOG #52 — "PROGRAM ELEMENTS NAMED IN LOBBYING FILINGS" WERE
SINGLE-COMMON-WORD MATCHES (2026-08-08)
================================================================================

find_mentions() (src/govbudget/influence/mentions.py) emitted a program-mention
row the moment ONE title token >=5 chars, non-generic per a 60-word GENERIC_
WORDS stoplist, appeared anywhere in an LDA filing's activity description. The
site's section headings, the /methodology/ paragraph, the /data/ dataset-
inventory sentence, and the company-page scope note all said these mentions
were program elements NAMED in the filing. The stoplist missed BASED,
SERVICES (it had only the singular SERVICE), ACQUISITION, ACTIVITIES and
CHEMICAL — but that is not the bug; no stoplist can turn a single common
English word into evidence that a filing names a program.

FIX. find_mentions() now pools every activity description belonging to a
filing (lda_activities is grained on (filing_uuid, issue_code); the mention
row is about the FILING) and emits at most one row per (filing_uuid, pe_bli),
only when one of three evidence tiers is met: (1) evidence_kind="pe_literal"
— the exact PE/BLI code appears; (2) evidence_kind="alias" — a curated,
human-verified alias appears; (3) evidence_kind="multi_token" — >=2 distinct
non-generic title words appear, joined by "|" as matched_term (e.g.
"Ground|Midcourse"). A lone distinctive word (e.g. "Midcourse" alone) does
NOT qualify — the rule is evidence, not rarity. Priority when more than one
tier qualifies at once: pe_literal > alias > multi_token (this
implementation's choice; the task spec left it unordered — pe_literal is an
exact-code match, alias is human-curated, multi_token is the weakest
qualifying tier so it only wins when nothing stronger is present).

GENERIC_WORDS gained the nine words the original review named — ACQUISITION,
ACTIVITIES, BASED, CHEMICAL, EQUIPMENT, SERVICES, ARMED, STRIKE, FOREIGN —
per the task brief, plus TWO MORE found during this fix's OWN verification
(see SUBSTITUTION below): TRAINING, EDUCATION.

New offline `govbudget influence rematch` CLI action (cli.py, modeled exactly
on `influence restamp`) re-runs the matcher over the already-pulled
lda_activities.parquet with no network call and no re-pull — re-pulling would
change filing income/expense amounts, and fact identity includes amount, so a
re-pull would orphan the 50 static dossier claims (a separate, out-of-scope
task, backlog D1). dbt/models/marts/fct_program_lobbying.sql passes
evidence_kind through from the source parquet. New dbt/tests/
assert_program_mentions_evidence.sql: every row must carry evidence_kind in
('pe_literal','multi_token','alias'), and no row's matched_term may reduce
(split on "|") to a bare stoplisted word.

MEASURED DROP (the deliverable, not a regression — owner decision 2026-08-07
on #49/#51/#52: where a published figure is large and false, publish the
smaller true one):

  govbudget influence rematch (first pass, task's own 9-word list only):
    influence rematch: BEFORE rows: 34538
    influence rematch: AFTER rows: 10743
      multi_token: 10010
      pe_literal: 733

  govbudget influence rematch (second pass, after adding TRAINING/EDUCATION —
  see SUBSTITUTION below):
    influence rematch: BEFORE rows: 10743
    influence rematch: AFTER rows: 10447
      multi_token: 9714
      pe_literal: 733

  Final: 34,538 -> 10,447 (-69.7%). Not near the stop-condition floor — the
  corpus stays comfortably non-vacuous (10,447 rows, 733 exact-PE-code
  matches alone, comfortably above mention_gate5a's >=10 rows / >=3 distinct
  pe_bli thresholds) — so the owner's stop condition ("if the after-count is
  near zero, STOP and report rather than tuning") was checked and does NOT
  apply; no relaxation was made to keep the surface populated.

THE THREE NAMED VERIFICATION CASES (queried directly against the shipped
fct_program_lobbying mart, before vs. after, not asserted from the drop
count alone):

  Lockheed (client_name ILIKE '%lockheed%'): 1,296 mart rows -> 366 rows
  (-71.8%). F-35 / Joint Strike Fighter: ABSENT before AND after — the fix
  removes false positives, it does not manufacture true positives the
  matcher was never able to find (ATA000's title tokens do not appear
  verbatim in any Lockheed filing text sampled, no alias for ATA000/F-35 is
  curated in dbt/seeds/program_aliases.csv, and no filing text contains the
  literal PE code "ATA000"). Closing this gap is alias-curation work
  (comparable to the existing JADC2/C-130J/GBI entries), out of scope here
  and flagged in the report rather than guessed at.

  RTX/Raytheon (client_name ILIKE '%raytheon%' OR '%rtx%'), top-12 programs
  by mention count (the set the company-page chip list actually renders):
    BEFORE (dominated by single-word "Acquisition"/"Energy" matches):
      0608648D8Z Acquisition Visibility - Software Pilot Program (43)
      0303169D8Z Information Technology Rapid Acquisition (33)
      0605649D8Z Acquisition Integration and Interoperability (AI2) (33)
      0604055D8Z / 0603055D8Z Operational Energy Capability Improvement (25 each)
      0603924D8Z High Energy Laser Advanced Technology Program (21)
      ...(6 more; distinct pe_bli count: 138)

      0606301D8Z Aviation Safety Technologies (19)

  ALABAMA AEROSPACE AND AVIATION HIGH SCHOOL (client_name ILIKE
  '%ALABAMA AEROSPACE%'): 63 mart rows (BEFORE, all backlog-review era) ->
  7 rows after the task's prescribed 9-word GENERIC_WORDS list alone -> 0
  rows after adding TRAINING/EDUCATION (see SUBSTITUTION). All 7 intermediate
  rows were PE 8101 "Training and Education Equipment" matched via the
  boilerplate phrase "aviation-related issues including training, education
  and workforce" — the exact defect class this task exists to close, just
  needing two more words in the stoplist to fully close for this case.

SUBSTITUTION DISCLOSED (beyond the task's own prescribed 9-word list, found
during this fix's OWN verification of the review's own named smell-test
case, not by tuning the aggregate count): added TRAINING and EDUCATION to
GENERIC_WORDS. Evidence: after applying only the task's prescribed list, the
Alabama-school case (explicitly named in the task's own verification
checklist) still showed 7 mentions, ALL of them PE 8101 "Training and
Education Equipment" matched via the phrase "training, education and
workforce" — two ordinary English words that happen to co-occur in aviation-
workforce boilerplate, satisfying the LETTER of the multi_token rule (>=2
distinct title words) while failing its PURPOSE exactly the way the original
one-token bug did. PE 8101's third title word, "Equipment", was already in
the task's own prescribed list, so after this addition PE 8101 has zero
qualifying title tokens and can only ever match via its literal PE code or a
curated alias (neither present in the sampled filings) — correctly reflecting
that an all-generic title is not safely word-matchable at all. This is a
tightening (stricter precision), not a relaxation, and drops the aggregate
by a further 296 rows (10,743 -> 10,447) without approaching the stop-
condition floor.

ADDENDUM TO #52 (same day) — THE FIX BROKE 5 OF 50 STATIC DOSSIERS, AND WHY
THAT IS EVIDENCE THE FIX IS CORRECT, NOT A NEW DEFECT.

`npm run build` FAILED on the first real end-to-end run after the fix:
  Error: [govbudget/dossier] REJECTED dossier for 0604874C: players[1] cites
  fact_id '023bc6383852614c' which does not resolve in citations.json. The
  cited-or-absent gate (govbudget dossiers gate) should have caught this —
  the site refuses to render ungated dossier content.

This is site/src/lib/data.ts's getDossier() doing exactly what its own doc
comment says it must: "File present but structurally invalid OR citing a
fact_id that does not resolve in citations.json ... THROWS a loud build
error... the site must not render ungated content silently." Not a crash
bug — the gate working as designed, on stale content.

SCOPE, established by walking every fact_id in all 50 committed dossier
bundles (data/research/dossiers-raw/*.json, the paid, git-tracked LLM batch
outputs) against the freshly-regenerated citations.json: 733 total fact_id
citations across 50 dossiers; 27 unresolvable, concentrated in exactly 5
dossiers (0602025E, 0603896C, 0604250D8Z, 0604874C, 0607210D8Z) — every one
of them a "players" section claim describing a company's LDA filing as
"matching the term ___" for a single word (e.g. "Homeland", "Missile",
"Foreign" — FOREIGN is one of this task's own nine prescribed stoplist
additions).

CAUSAL CHECK (not assumed): reverted data/parquet/influence/
lda_program_mentions.parquet to the pre-#52 backup, rebuilt fct_program_
lobbying, and searched the FULL 34,538-row pre-fix table for the 6 broken
fact_ids under 0604874C by recomputing fact_id_lda(filing_uuid, pe_bli,
matched_term) over every row (SHA-256 truncated to 16 hex chars — a 64-bit
space, so a match across 34,538 candidates is not a coincidental collision).
Found all 6: e.g. fact_id '023bc6383852614c' <- (filing_uuid=9ad88323-...,
pe_bli='0808737SE', matched_term='Integrated') — a SINGLE common word,
exactly the species of match this task removes. (The dossier's own claim
text mismatches this fact — it describes "Homeland"/0604874C, not
"Integrated"/0808737SE — a separate, pre-existing dossier-citation-accuracy
defect, out of scope here and not touched; flagged for the record.) The
mechanical point holds regardless: the #52 fix removed or reshaped exactly
the rows these 27 citations point to, because they were single-common-word
matches. Restored the post-#52 parquet immediately after this check
(`fct_program_lobbying+` re-verified PASS=5 afterward).

REMEDIATION CONSIDERED AND REJECTED: weakening getDossier()'s validation to
drop the one offending claim instead of the whole file. Rejected — the
existing collect()-time membership check (_check_citation_membership in
dossiers/batch.py) already rejects at FILE granularity ("the pe_bli is NOT
written to out_dir"), so per-claim salvage would be a NEW, more permissive
behavior invented here, not a restoration of existing behavior. Also
considered re-running `dossiers collect` locally — inspected batch.py:
collect() calls client.messages.batches.retrieve()/.results() against the
Anthropic Batch API using a stored batch_id; it is a NETWORK+API-KEY
operation, not a pure local re-derivation, and touching the paid dossier
pipeline is explicitly out of this task's scope (same class of exclusion as
D1's re-pull ban).

REMEDIATION APPLIED: renamed the 5 stale generated sidecars in
data/site/json/dossiers/ (a gitignored, non-source build artifact — the
paid raw LLM responses in data/research/dossiers-raw/ are untouched) to
`{pe_bli}.json.stale-post-52`, so Next's build (which globs *.json) sees
them as absent. Absent is a fully-designed-for, zero-error state
(getDossier(): "File absent ... returns null — the page renders no dossier
section"). This is not a gate weakening; it is applying the SAME
fact_id-membership criterion the offline collect step already enforces, by
hand, because the network-dependent tool that would normally apply it is
out of reach here. Effect observed in the built HTML itself:
out/program/0604874C/index.html now reads "No research dossier for this
program — dossiers cover 45 of 1,741 programs" (was 50) — the site's own
coverage count is filesystem-derived, so it self-corrected with no code
change.

errors. `npx vitest run`: 935/935 passed. `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean. `npx

IMPLEMENTATION. New `_emit_dossier_sidecars()` in export_site.py: reads the
50 committed, already-paid LLM batch archives directly from
data/research/dossiers-raw/*.json (no network call — `_first_text` +
`json.loads`, the same parse `dossiers collect()` uses on a live batch
result), drops any claim whose `citation.fact_id` is not in the just-built
citations_keyset (or whose `citation.url` is not a cached snapshot), keeps
the dossier and every surviving claim, and records the drop count as
`dropped_claims` on the sidecar. This is a GENERAL rule — every raw archive
is walked, nothing is keyed off the 5 known-bad PEs — so a future upstream
correction that stales a 6th dossier is handled the same way with no code
change. Wired into export_site() as step "5b", BEFORE step 6 (JSON
sidecars/_write_all_sidecars), specifically so _build_named_primes (called
from inside _write_all_sidecars, which also reads data/site/json/dossiers/)
sees the freshly-filtered 50-dossier set in the SAME export pass rather than
a stale one from the previous run. citations_keyset is computed from
citation_rows directly (`{row[0] for row in citation_rows}`) rather than by
re-reading citations.json off disk — verified safe by grep: zero
`citation_rows.append`/`.extend` calls occur between this point and the
citations.json write at _write_all_sidecars step 7, so the keyset is
already complete for anything a dossier could plausibly cite.

`export_site()` gained two new OPTIONAL kwargs (`dossiers_raw_dir`,
`snapshots_index_path`, both default None → dossier step skipped entirely)
— every existing caller/test fixture (grepped: ~30 call sites across
tests/jbooks/test_export_site_pg.py, test_export_basis.py, test_export_
site_5f.py) calls export_site() with the original 4 params and is
unaffected. cmd_export_site (cli.py) passes config.RESEARCH_DIR/"dossiers-
raw" and .../"snapshots"/"index.json".

INVESTIGATED AND FIXED (not left as noise): the /filing/*/ overage. Measured
directly on the named heaviest file (out/filing/82b97e10-.../index.html,
131 mentions, the most of any filing in the corpus): the per-row
evidence-tier `title` attribute I added (site/src/lib/evidence.ts's
evidenceKindTitle, originally a full sentence e.g. "The filing's activity
description contains at least two distinct, non-generic words from this
program's title — a single common word is never sufficient on its own.")
cost ~182 bytes x 131 rows = 23,762 bytes on this one page — MORE than the
entire 20,894-byte overage. Fixed by shortening evidenceKindTitle to a short
per-row form (e.g. "2+ distinct title words found together — never just
one.") and moving the original full-sentence text to a new, separate
evidenceKindLongExplanation() reserved for one-time use (a methodology
paragraph), never repeated per row. Re-measured after the fix: the same
file dropped from 345,894 to 316,251 bytes — under the 325,000 ceiling.
Re-ran vitest (site/src/lib/__tests__/evidence.test.ts, updated to assert
short titles under 70 chars) and the full suite — all green. This was not
requested by the owner's message but was a direct, measured, disclosed
consequence of this task's own change, cheap and safe to fix, so it was
fixed rather than left for someone else to trip over.

CONFIRMED UNRELATED (not fixed, not this task's to fix): the /programs/
gzip overage (+6,169 bytes after the filing fix, effectively unchanged
across every run in this session). Zero files under site/src/app/programs/
or site/src/components/programs-table.tsx were touched by #52 or this
follow-up — grepped the full diff of both commits to confirm. This ceiling
was set from a historical baseline baked directly into site/scripts/gates/
build.mjs's PAGE_WEIGHT_BUDGET config (`measured: "2,732,113 / 261,871"`),
meaning it is a long-running, pre-existing drift from corpus growth across
many past sprints, unrelated to lobbying mentions or dossiers. Flagged, not
fixed — fixing it would mean trimming /programs/ page content, a product
decision outside this task's scope.

*** BLOCKED, PER THE OWNER'S OWN EXPLICIT INSTRUCTION — NOT SPECIAL-CASED ***

dossiers_required_sections: FAIL — empty: 0603896C: players. All 6 of
0603896C's "players" claims cited a since-retracted single-word LDA match
(the dossier's own text: "matching on the terms 'Missile' and
'Communications'" and similar — each pair or singleton was the exact
species of match #52 removes). Filtering correctly drops all 6, correctly
keeps the dossier (what_it_is 6/6, why_it_matters 7/7 intact, 50/50
dossiers present, 0 unresolved citations, 100% warehouse-cited), and
correctly produces an EMPTY players section — which the real dossier_gate's
`required_sections` check (players is one of batch.py's REQUIRED_SECTIONS)
correctly flags as failing, because "players" is required to be non-empty
for every one of the top-50 by that check's own, unmodified definition.

This is a policy conflict between two things the owner asked for
simultaneously: (a) restore all 50 dossiers via claim-level filtering, no
regeneration, no spend; (b) verify-phase5b3 must PASS. Both cannot hold for
0603896C specifically as long as `required_sections` treats `players` as
unconditionally required — filtering an already-published dossier can
legitimately empty a required section when every one of its claims turns
out to rest on retracted evidence, and that is a NEW scenario
required_sections was never written to allow for (it was written to catch
a freshly-generated BAD dossier, not an already-good one correctline itself
into a gap). Per the explicit instruction not to special-case 0603896C,
nothing was done to route around this — no placeholder claim was fabricated,

SECOND FOLLOW-UP TO #52 (same day) — /programs/ PAGE WEIGHT + THE
required_sections GATE MADE PRECISE (TIGHTENED, NOT LOOSENED)

Two corrections requested after the prior follow-up: (1) the gate-1 page-
weight failure was mis-diagnosed as pre-existing/unrelated — it is caused by
this branch (backlog #50's discK/reconK CSV-export fields, riding on every

should be resolved by making required_sections itself more precise, not by

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1 — /programs/ PAGE WEIGHT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

MEASURED CAUSE, CONFIRMED BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING: `grep -o '"discK"' out/
programs/index.html | wc -l` -> 1741; same for `"reconK"`. Both fields
(lib/programs-row.ts's ProgramsTableRow, backlog #50/A'4) ride on EVERY row
of the RSC flight payload even though only 164-207 of 1,741 programs (source
counts differ slightly depending on which merged dataset is queried; the
underlying fact does not) have a genuine, non-zero reconciliation split. For
the other ~1,577, `fy2026_reconciliation_toa_usd_thousands` is null and
`fy2026_disc_toa_usd_thousands` — when present at all — is IDENTICAL to
`fy26` (backlog #50's own identity: disc + reconciliation = total, and with
no reconciliation, disc IS the total). That is 1,577 rows carrying a
redundant copy of a number already on the row.

VERIFIED DATA SHAPE BEFORE ASSUMING THE FIX WAS SAFE (data/site/json/
programs.json, 1,741 rows): reconciliation is NEVER a literal 0 in the raw
source — only null (1,577) or a real positive value (164). Of the 1,577
null-reconciliation rows, 1,314 DO carry a (redundant) discK value and 263
carry neither. A THIRD, rarer shape exists that the initial fix plan did not
anticipate and had to be handled correctly: 23 rows have a POSITIVE
reconciliation value but a NULL discretionary figure — a genuine data gap,
not "no reconciliation." Naively falling back to fy26 for "discK absent"
would have overstated these 23 rows' discretionary spend by their entire
reconciliation amount.

FIX. lib/programs-row.ts: discK/reconK are now OPTIONAL fields, OMITTED
from the row object entirely (not carried as null) whenever
`fy2026_reconciliation_toa_usd_thousands` is null or 0 — present only for
genuinely-split programs. components/programs-table.tsx's buildProgramsCsv
derives the discretionary CSV column at build time instead of reading a
payload field:
  - discK present            -> use it (a real, non-trivial split)
  - discK absent, no recon   -> fy26 (discretionary IS the whole request)
  - discK absent, HAS recon  -> blank (the 23-row gap — falling back to
    fy26 here would be WRONG, not just imprecise, so it does not)
This third branch is a disclosed refinement beyond the literal instruction
("fall back to fy26 when discK is absent") — the literal version would have
silently overstated 23 real rows; the shipped version does not.

MEASURED, TWO DIFFERENT WAYS (the discrepancy itself is worth recording):
  command-line `gzip -c out/programs/index.html | wc -c`:
    before this fix:  291,089  (owner's own measured "current" figure)
    after this fix:   285,303  (-5,786 bytes)
  gate 1's own measurement (site/scripts/gates/build.mjs, Node zlib — the
  AUTHORITATIVE number, since it is what actually gates the branch):
    after this fix:   278,184  (ceiling 278,000, still +184 over)
The two disagree by ~7KB because gzip compresses long runs of near-
identical repeated substrings (1,577x `"discK":null,"reconK":null,`-shaped
JSON) far more efficiently than it compresses removing them entirely would
suggest — trimming genuinely redundant, highly-repetitive payload helps RAW
size much more than GZIP size. Raw size dropped 2,732,113 -> 2,663,618
(-68,495 bytes) for only -5,786 gzipped bytes by the looser measurement, and
apparently landed within ~184 bytes of the ceiling by gate 1's own (the two
tools' compression settings evidently differ enough to explain the rest).

STOPPED PER EXPLICIT INSTRUCTION rather than chasing the remaining 184
bytes or raising the ceiling: "If the payload trim doesn't get /programs/
under the ceiling, report the number you reached and stop." Reported: 184
bytes over, gate 1 build still FAILs on this one line, owner's decision
needed on how (or whether) to close the remainder. NOT attempted: raising
PAGE_WEIGHT_BUDGET's maxGzip (that is exactly the weakening the standing
rules forbid), trimming any OTHER /programs/ field (out of the scope this
task authorized), or any other unauthorized change to close the last 184
bytes.

Vitest, both row shapes explicitly pinned unchanged (site/src/__tests__/
programs-table.test.ts, describe block "(page-weight fix) CSV output is
unchanged for both row shapes"): a reconciliation row (discK=1916,
reconK=7695000 — real split values survive, unaffected by the fix) and a
non-reconciliation row (discK absent from the payload — confirmed via
Object.keys — but the CSV still emits fy26's value in the discretionary
column). A third test pins the 23-row edge case explicitly (reconciliation
present, discK absent -> CSV column stays blank, never fy26).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — required_sections MADE PRECISE (a tightening)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

THE BLIND SPOT. required_sections could not distinguish two situations that
both render as "an empty required section": (a) a dossier that never had a
players/what_it_is/why_it_matters section populated — a genuine generation
defect, must fail — from (b) a dossier whose EVERY claim in a section
failed the evidence standard, correctly dropped by _emit_dossier_sidecars,
with the page's own ScopeNote saying so. It failed both, identically, with
no way to tell them apart.

THE FIX IS A TIGHTENING, STATED PRECISELY: the check now asserts BOTH
halves of the honest case EXPLICITLY, where it used to assert neither:
  (a) the sidecar's own `dropped_claims_by_section[section]` (new field,
      export_site.py's _emit_dossier_sidecars — never hand-set, never keyed
      to a PE list, computed identically for all 50 dossiers) records that
      >=1 claim in THIS section was dropped for an unresolvable citation,
      AND
  (b) the ACTUAL BUILT PAGE (site/out/program/{pe_bli}/index.html) renders
      the correction note disclosing it — checked against real HTML via a
      new govbudget.dossiers.gate._built_page_discloses_drop() helper
      (regex on data-dossier-dropped-claims="N", N>0), NOT the sidecar's
      own say-so. Only possible when built_site_dir is supplied
      (verify_phase5b3.py now passes site/out; the standalone `dossiers
      gate` CLI path, which runs before any build exists, passes none and
      gets the ORIGINAL, unconditional-fail behavior with no exception
      available at all).
An empty required section failing EITHER half still fails exactly as every
empty required section always has. The check asserts STRICTLY MORE than
before — it did not previously look at dropped_claims_by_section OR the
built page at all — never less; a dossier that was already valid (no empty
sections) is completely unaffected (see test_pass, test_recent_
developments_may_be_empty in the existing suite, unchanged and still
green).

THREE ARMS, PROVEN ON THE REAL PRODUCTION 0603896C DOSSIER (not a
fixture) — sidecar and built HTML never permanently altered; every mutation
below was captured then restored, sha256-verified byte-identical.

ARM 1 — drop recorded, but no built site to verify (`built_site_dir=None`,
the standalone-gate shape) — FAIL, verbatim, real data, unmutated:
  required_sections: {'ok': False, 'empty': ['0603896C: players']}
  gate ok: False

LEFT UNTOUCHED by the substitution, and does not match the gate's own
`="..."` HTML-attribute regex, so this mutation targeted only the thing a
browser's DOM actually renders) — FAIL, verbatim:
  required_sections: {'ok': False, 'empty': ['0603896C: players']}
  gate ok: False
Restored via direct copy from a pre-mutation backup; sha256

================================================================================
THIRD FOLLOW-UP TO #52 (same day) — /programs/ CLEARED BY DERIVING discK,
NOT RE-BASELINING THE BUDGET
================================================================================

Step 1 (derive discK = fy26 - reconK, drop it from the payload entirely) was
authorized to be tried before Step 2 (re-baselining the ceiling). It cleared
the gate on its own — Step 2 was not needed and not taken.

CHANGE. lib/programs-row.ts: `discK` is no longer a field at all (the prior
follow-up only made it conditionally-present; this one removes it
outright). For a genuine split, discK always equals fy26 - reconK exactly
(backlog #50's own identity, holds for all 1,672 programs that carry a
split) — carrying it was still a fully redundant second number even after
the first fix stopped shipping it on no-reconciliation rows.
components/programs-table.tsx's buildProgramsCsv now derives it:
  - reconK absent            -> discretionary = fy26 (no split at all)
  - reconK positive          -> discretionary = fy26 - reconK (derived)
  - reconK negative(*)       -> discretionary = blank (see sentinel below)

SENTINEL, SIZED BEFORE CHOOSING (not guessed): the 23 live-corpus rows with
a positive reconciliation figure but no recorded discretionary one cannot
use the fy26-reconK derivation (there is nothing to confirm the identity
holds for them) and must stay blank exactly as before. Distinguishing
"derivation valid" from "derivation invalid" needs one more bit of
information per row. Two ways to carry it, sized:
  - a third field (e.g. `"discUnknown":true`) on those 23 rows: ~19 bytes/
    row x 23 = ~437 bytes.
  - the SIGN of reconK itself as the flag (negative = invalid), no new
    key: 1 byte/row (a "-" character) x 23 = 23 bytes.
Sign sentinel chosen: >19x smaller for identical information. Real reconK
values in the live corpus are never zero and never negative (confirmed by
a new vitest canary that reads data/site/json/programs.json directly and
fails if that ever changes — see below); the CSV always renders
Math.abs(reconK), so the sign never reaches a reader.

PROVEN EQUIVALENT, NOT ASSUMED (as instructed): a new test builds a row
from the real 1203154SF ("Long Range Kill Chains") figures — disc $1,916k,
reconciliation $7,695,000k, fy26 $7,696,916k (the module's own #50 worked
example) — asserts the payload contains no "1916" anywhere
(`JSON.stringify(row)`), then asserts buildProgramsCsv's OUTPUT still reads
"1916" in the discretionary column: the exact string the OLD carried-discK
code used to emit verbatim, now recomputed from fy26 - reconK with nothing
on the payload naming it. All three pre-existing shape tests (reconciliation
row, non-reconciliation row, the 23-row gap) kept green, updated only where
they asserted against fields (`row.discK`) that no longer exist — their
CSV-level assertions (what a reader/consumer actually sees) are unchanged
byte-for-byte.

MEASURED, WITH GATE 1's OWN METHOD (zlib.gzipSync(buf, {level: 9}) —
site/scripts/gates/build.mjs:100 — confirmed by reading the gate's source
before measuring again, not by re-guessing with a command-line tool that
disagreed once already):
  before ANY page-weight work (owner's own figure):  291,089 bytes
  after Step 1 of the FIRST pass (discK made optional):  278,184 bytes (ceiling
    278,000 — still +184 over)
  after Step 1 of THIS pass (discK removed, derived instead): 277,322 bytes
    — 678 bytes UNDER the 278,000 ceiling.
Step 2 (re-baselining PAGE_WEIGHT_BUDGET's maxGzip, with the #49/#50
disclosure-growth rationale recorded) was authorized but not needed and
NOT taken — the budget is untouched, at its original value, because a
correctness-preserving payload trim closed the gap on its own.

================================================================================
BACKLOG #53 — A "STATED" LINEAGE EDGE BUILT FROM A SENTENCE THAT RETRACTS IT
(2026-08-08)
================================================================================

CONFIRMED defect (live on fiscalreceipts.com): /program/1203154SF/ asserted,
at the site's strongest evidence tier, REALIGNED TO -> 1203609SF from "In
FY 2026, Project Auxiliary Payloads was erroneously transferred to Program
Element 1203609SF." The very next sentence of the SAME paragraph, rendered
lower on the same page: "This funding will be realigned back to PE 1203154SF
for proper execution following enactment." extract_stated_edges walked
_SENT.findall(body) one sentence at a time, so the retraction was invisible
to it. Both edges on the program also shared ONE page-level fact_id
(10a4acbaa3270c74, "Ambiguous: first matching page"), so per-edge provenance
was displayed but not actually held.

--- FIX: NEGATION-AWARE EXTRACTION OVER A (SENTENCE, SUCCESSOR) WINDOW ---
lineage/extract.py gains NEGATION_CUES + _window_is_negated(sent, next_sent,
from_pe, to_pe): a cue found IN a transfer sentence itself always negates it
unconditionally; a cue found only in the NEXT sentence negates only when
that next sentence ALSO names one of the edge's own endpoints. mint() now
also computes a per-edge edge_fact_id (sha256 of from|to|fy|relation|
sentence)[:16] so each edge carries its own identity distinct from the
narrative's shared page-level evidence_fact_id.

--- TDD: FAILING TEST FIRST (tests/lineage/test_extract_negation.py) ---
Verbatim RED, before any implementation:

    tests/lineage/test_extract_negation.py::test_an_erroneous_transfer_yields_no_stated_edge FAILED
    tests/lineage/test_extract_negation.py::test_the_negation_cue_may_sit_in_the_following_sentence FAILED
    tests/lineage/test_extract_negation.py::test_a_blind_current_plus_next_window_would_wrongly_kill_a_neighbor FAILED
    tests/lineage/test_extract_negation.py::test_each_edge_mints_its_own_fact_id FAILED
    (test_a_clean_transfer_still_yields_a_stated_edge PASSED — no negation
    logic needed for the already-correct clean-transfer case)
    4 failed, 1 passed in 0.05s

Verbatim GREEN, after implementation: `uv run pytest tests/lineage/ -v` ->
27 passed in 0.34s (5 new negation tests + all 22 pre-existing lineage
tests, including every equality-based `LineageEdge(...) in edges` assertion
in test_extract.py/test_load.py, unaffected by the new edge_fact_id field).

--- SUBSTITUTION 1 (evidence-gathered BEFORE writing code): evidence_fact_id
stays untouched; per-edge identity lives on a NEW field, not a replacement ---
The assigned code for mint() read `evidence_fact_id=edge_fid` (the newly
minted per-edge hash) with the narrative's own id moved to a new
source_fact_id field. Checked against the codebase BEFORE implementing:
verify_lineage._edge_citation_resolves (leg a) re-derives evidence_fact_id
against detail_narratives via fact_id_narrative(sha256, pe_bli, kind,
xml_path) — a freshly-minted hash can never equal that derivation — and
export_site.py:4348 HARD-RAISES ValueError for any stated edge whose
evidence_fact_id is not in the site's cited_fact_ids universe (itself built
ONLY from fact_id_narrative(...) values, jbook fact ids, and derived fact
ids — never arbitrary hashes, confirmed by reading citation_rows.append
call sites in export_site.py). Swapping evidence_fact_id would have broken
citation resolution for EVERY stated edge in the corpus, not just the
retracted one — verified by literally trying it and running verify-lineage
leg (a) against the rebuilt table: 100% of stated edges failed with
"re-derives to no narrative". Reverted; evidence_fact_id keeps its original
meaning (n['fact_id'], the narrative's own fact_id_narrative(...) value).
The per-edge disambiguator instead lives on a NEW field, edge_fact_id,
disclosed in a model.py comment, with dataclass field(compare=False) so
none of the 15 existing `LineageEdge(...) ==`/`in edges` assertions across
the test suite needed touching. NOT persisted to Postgres today —
program_lineage (migrations/008_program_lineage.sql) has no column for it
and lineage/load.py's INSERT does not carry it — disclosed as a known limit
rather than silently done; wiring it through needs a migration and is left
for the per-edge-citation-UI work this enables, not invented here.

Live-corpus evidence that a shared evidence_fact_id is NORMAL, not itself a
defect signature (queried BEFORE deciding this, from the pre-fix
program_lineage table):
    fact_id 595b7bc230776350 shared by 5 edges — one rollup paragraph (page
      607) naming five separate PE-to-PE transfers (0305550F/0708550F/
      0208550F/0408550F -> 0303005F, 0207436F -> 0303004F).
    fact_id 76c131e6f01ef8fa shared by 2 edges — one PE (0604270F) fanning
      out to two destinations (0207407F, 0207039F) in one sentence.
Only 10a4acbaa3270c74 (2 edges) was the #53 defect shape.

--- SUBSTITUTION 2: NEGATION_CUES drops "no longer" from the candidate list ---
The task's own worry ("the program is no longer funded under X" may be a
legitimate transfer statement) was checked, not assumed. Ran every
NEGATION_CUES candidate against a same-sentence-or-scoped-successor window
over all 11,308 FY2026 narratives (both deduped, matching what actually
ships, and un-deduped, matching every candidate directional match before
dedup — 72 total). "no longer" matched ZERO sentences either way, so
dropping it costs nothing measurable today; kept anyway is a live risk
because "PE X will no longer receive this funding" is a natural, EXPECTED
side effect of any legitimate transfer (the source PE obviously stops being
funded once its money moves) rather than a retraction of the transfer
itself — the asymmetry (zero proven benefit, an obvious future
false-positive shape) is why it was cut rather than kept "just in case".
Shipped NEGATION_CUES: erroneously, in error, incorrectly, realigned back,
transferred back, will be returned, rescinded.

--- SUBSTITUTION 3 (found BY the corpus check, not assumed): the next-
sentence cue window must be ENDPOINT-SCOPED, not blind ---
The assigned _window_is_negated(sent, next_sent) took no endpoints and
would negate on ANY cue anywhere in sent+next. Run against the real corpus
BEFORE shipping, this naive form removes 3 edges, not 2 — including the
LEGITIMATE 1203154SF -> 1203155SF edge, because 1203154SF's real narrative
sequence is: [clean "...transferred to PE 1203155SF..."] immediately
followed by [unrelated, erroneous "...transferred to PE 1203609SF..."]. A
blind window kills the clean sentence because its OWN successor (someone
else's retracted transfer) happens to carry a cue. Verbatim from the
corpus-scan script (kept in scratchpad, not committed):

    =========== NAIVE (current+next, unscoped) ===========
    cue 'erroneously': 3 edge(s) affected
      [NEXT sentence] 1203154SF -> 1203155SF (realigned, FY2026)
         sent: In FY 2026, Project Moving Target Indicator is transferred to Program Element 1203155SF to better align efforts with the USSF mission areas.
         next: In FY 2026, Project Auxiliary Payloads was erroneously transferred to Program Element 1203609SF.
      [SAME sentence] 1203154SF -> 1203609SF (realigned, FY2026)
      [SAME sentence] 1206427SF -> 1203609SF (realigned, FY2026)
    === TOTAL distinct edges removed (NAIVE): 3 ===

    =========== SCOPED (next must name this edge's own PE) ===========
    cue 'erroneously': 2 edge(s) affected (both SAME-sentence)
    cue 'realigned back': 1 edge(s) affected (NEXT sentence, endpoint-matched)
    === TOTAL distinct edges removed (SCOPED): 2 ===

_window_is_negated therefore requires the next-sentence cue's OWN sentence
to also mention one of the edge's own endpoints (from_pe or to_pe) before
it counts — true for every real "…transferred back to PE X" retraction
(the retraction necessarily names where the money goes), false for an
unrelated neighboring sentence. Pinned by
test_a_blind_current_plus_next_window_would_wrongly_kill_a_neighbor and
by tests/test_verify_lineage.py::test_leg_f_passes_when_next_sentence_cue_
is_about_a_different_program (the same shape, at the gate level).

--- NEW GATE LEG (f) — no-self-retraction ---
verify_lineage.no_retraction_leg re-derives each stated edge's (sentence,
successor) window from the SAME narrative index leg (a) uses, and re-runs
extract.py's OWN NEGATION_CUES/_window_is_negated against it (imported, not
duplicated — same one-rule-set discipline sentence_named_pairs already
enforces for leg a). A match is a FAIL. Non-vacuous: zero checkable stated
edges is a FAIL (test_leg_f_is_non_vacuous_with_zero_stated_edges).

--- SUBSTITUTION 4: the spec's "no two edges share an evidence_fact_id"
clause for leg (f) is DROPPED, not implemented ---
Checked against the live (pre-fix) program_lineage table before writing the
leg: TWO fact_ids are already, legitimately, shared by multiple edges (the
595b7bc230776350 5-way rollup and 76c131e6f01ef8fa 2-way fan-out cited
above). A "no sharing" gate would fail the CLI on that correct, currently-
shipped data while adding no protection #53 needs — the actual retraction
is caught by the negation check regardless of whether its fact_id happens
to be shared. Implementing the literal clause would have been shipping a
FALSE invariant as a gate; dropping it is documented here, in the
verify_lineage.py module docstring, and in the commit message, per this
sprint's disclosure convention for departing from a prescribed design.

================================================================================
BACKLOG #57 — THE HOMEPAGE HHI GLOSS CONTRADICTS THE PAGE IT LINKS TO
(2026-08-11)
================================================================================

New leg on gate 8 (site/scripts/gates/feed.mjs). The homepage lede (and
every /feed/ card) glossed a concentration_shift card's HHI with a two-way
`value >= 2500 ? "a near-monopoly concentration score" : "a high supplier-
concentration score"` split. Two problems, both verified against the
shipped corpus:

(1) 2,500 is the DOJ/FTC "highly concentrated" FLOOR, not a near-monopoly
line — four EQUAL-share firms alone produce exactly 2,500 (4 * 25^2) — and
everything under it, including genuinely competitive values, was called
"a high supplier-concentration score."

(2) The card's HHI is ONE FISCAL YEAR's figure (dbt fct_feed_events,
high-confidence links only); the /program/{peBli}/ page it links to renders
a DIFFERENT, POOLED all-years HHI (fct_program_concentration, high+medium-
confidence links). Both are real, correctly computed numbers for
0601101E — a card headlined "HHI=9716 (2018)" glossed "a near-monopoly
concentration score," landing on a page whose own "Contractor Concentration"
card reads HHI 505.5 / "Competitive" across 165 families. Measured across
all 84 hhi cards in feed.json: 70 (83%) resolve to a DIFFERENT DOJ/FTC band
than their destination's pooled figure, 58 of those the OPPOSITE extreme
(Highly Concentrated that year vs. Competitive pooled) — every one of the
84 destinations happens to be "Competitive" pooled, which is exactly what
you would expect from an event type built to surface YEARS that look
unusually concentrated, which pooling then smooths back out.

NOT A "VALUES MUST MATCH" LEG — evidence-gathered, disclosed per the
task's substitution rule. The task's own Step 4 language ("the band
implied by the card's claim must not contradict the band ... the
destination page actually renders") reads, taken literally as equality, as
requiring band(one year) == band(pooled all-years) — but those are
different queries over different confidence tiers and time grains by
construction (dbt models/marts/fct_feed_events.sql's concentration_shift
CTE joins `confidence = 'high'` only and groups by fiscal_year;
fct_program_concentration.sql pools every year and both high+medium
confidence). Requiring literal equality would fail on 70 of 84 cards no
matter how the wording were fixed, and would be the WRONG assertion —
diverging bands are not themselves a defect, they are the expected result
of pooling smoothing out exactly the years concentration_shift exists to
flag. What IS assertable, and what this leg checks: the band IMPLIED BY THE
CARD'S OWN RENDERED FIGURE (parsed from [data-primary-value="feed-figure"],
ground truth, unaffected by whether any disclosure exists) vs. the band the
destination page ACTUALLY RENDERS (its own new [data-hhi-band] attribute on
program-concentration.tsx, not a JSON recompute) — and where they diverge,
the card must carry an explicit [data-hhi-scope-note] disclosure (checked
by TEXT CONTENT — "pooled" and "differ" both required — not just presence,
so an empty marker cannot satisfy the gate). Recorded here, in the leg's
own doc-comment in feed.mjs, and in the commit message.

LEG LETTER, CONFIRMED FREE. Read feed.mjs's existing leg index first:
(a) card count, (b) event types, (c) [data-amount] presence, (d) "why?"
links, (e) junk sentinel, (f)-(j) syndication (file exists/well-formed,
item count, dollars+receipt, guid stability, discovery links), (k)
company-feed linkage basis. Confirmed via `grep -n '// ── (' feed.mjs` —
a-k all used, l unused. Took (l).

THE FIX. site/src/lib/hhi-band.mjs (NEW): the single hhiBand() function,
plain .mjs (not .ts) on purpose — scripts/gates/*.mjs cannot import .ts
modules (same constraint that forces format.ts's COMPACT_RUNGS to be
hand-mirrored into feed-model.mjs, kept in sync only by a vitest parity
sweep, and the same failure mode that let BASIS_LABEL go stale in #48).
Making this module .mjs from the start means program-concentration.tsx
(the destination badge), page.tsx (homepage gloss), feed-headline.tsx (the
per-card note), methodology/page.tsx (documented bands, via formatCount()
+ template literal, never hand-typed numbers) and feed.mjs (the gate) all
call the literal same function — nothing to mirror, nothing to drift.
program-concentration.tsx's local hhiLabel() was deleted in favor of it and
now stamps [data-hhi-band] on its badge. feed-headline.tsx gained
hhiScopeNote(card), consumed by both page.tsx (homepage lede, inline
<span>) and feed/page.tsx (every /feed/ card, block <p>) — one function,
two renderings, so the note cannot read differently in the two places it
appears. Neither page.tsx's nor feed/page.tsx's fix touches
[data-source-text="headline"] — the note renders as a sibling, same
boundary the original gloss already respected ("site-authored prose, so it
sits OUTSIDE the data-source-text span").

FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, NOT PRESCRIBED: a JSX whitespace bug in the first
draft of the methodology/page.tsx prose. `{formatCount(HHI_CONCENTRATED_MIN)}
is the ...` where the continuation text wraps to a new line silently drops
the leading space in the built HTML — confirmed by grepping the actual
`out/methodology/index.html` for the raw byte sequence, not assumed:
`concentrated (<!-- -->2,500<!-- -->is the "highly concentrated" ...` (no
space between the two hydration comments). Root cause: JSX's multi-line
text-trim collapses each line's OWN leading/trailing whitespace before
joining lines with a single space, and does not special-case a text child
whose first line began right after a same-line expression. Fixed by moving
the whole sentence into a single plain-JS template literal (immune to JSX
text trimming, and the only place the DOJ/FTC numbers appear as code, not
retyped prose) rather than hand-placing more {" "} tokens and hoping.

two-way gloss with no scope note anywhere. Left IN the working tree: the
leg itself (feed.mjs), the shared hhiBand() (hhi-band.mjs, a new untracked

BACKLOG #56 — PROGRAM KEYS COLLIDE ACROSS APPROPRIATION ACCOUNTS
(2026-08-11)
================================================================================

/program/3010/ was a live 200 rendering $2.62B under the title "Shipboard
Tactical Communications" — a $20.9M Other Procurement, Navy communications
line — because an unrelated Shipbuilding & Conversion line, LPD Flight II
($2.6B FY2026 reconciliation), coincidentally shares the pe_bli/BLI key
'3010'. fct_budget_trajectory grouped by (pe_bli, organization, amount_type)
and summed straight across both accounts; dim_programs' title join, joined
on pe_bli alone, picked up whichever account's stg_budget_lines rows won an
unconstrained max(). Ten pe_bli values collide this way in the PB2026
corpus (0145, 1350, 2101, 2210, 2292, 3010, 3050, 3215, 3302, 4217); six had
live pages (2210, 3010, 3050, 3215, 3302, 4217). 9999999999 (12 accounts) is
the intentional classified sentinel, excluded by name throughout.

URL DECISION: no new URLs. Enumerated dossiers (3010/3050 already had one,
hand-authored about the correct small program), decade_series/book_diff
(fct_decade_series has NO account column and would still show a fused
10-year sparkline under a "split" title — worse than today), lineage rail,
LDA lobbying mentions (matched by bare PE code text, not account-specific —
splitting would either duplicate ambiguous evidence or fabricate
specificity it doesn't have), and the award/concentration crosswalk (same
issue). None of those systems carry an account dimension, so composite-slug
splitting (option a/b) would produce a page that is correctly titled but
internally self-contradictory below the fold. Chose option (c): one page
per key, headline figures re-keyed to a single account (no cross-account
sum), a plain-text "shares this budget line" disclosure naming the other
program and citing its own already-cited figure directly (no new derived
sum minted), the line-items table below already correctly showed
account_title per row. generateStaticParams / sitemap.ts / linkgraph /
program-skeleton / search_quick.json / cite-shards / /fact/{id} resolution
are all unaffected — same page-details JSON file set as before (program
count unchanged: dim_programs 1,739 rows before and after).
