--- ADDENDUM (same day): two more #56 leak points found via the FULL loop ---

Running the actual leg h implementation against a real build (not the
hand-traced examples above) surfaced two further leak points the initial
fix missed — both caught by re-running the loop end to end rather than
stopping at "leg h passes."

(1) LEG A2 (pre-existing, PM-review Sprint 1) still FAILED post-fix:

  X leg a2 collision (attribute path): 16 unreconciled (entity, fy, measure)
    collisions (first 10):
      program/3010/index.html: (3010|2026|request) renders 2 distinct
      values [$20.9M (basis=toa) vs $2.62B (basis=toa)] outside a
      [data-reconciliation] component
      program/2210/index.html: (2210|2026|request) renders 2 distinct
      values [$56.5M (basis=toa) vs $358.3M (basis=toa)] outside a
      [data-reconciliation] component
      ... (16 total, across all 8 pe_bli with a live page or decade grain)

Root cause: fct_decade_series (Phase 5E, spans all ten PB editions) is a
SEPARATE model from fct_budget_trajectory and was correctly left un-re-keyed
(its blast radius is out of this ticket's scope) — but it still fuses the
same two accounts, and it is NOT confined to the PB2026 edition: pe_bli
'3010' shows n_source_rows=2 in the PB2024 and PB2025 editions too (verified
directly against fct_decade_series). The decade sparkline / "asked vs
spent" book_diff on these program pages therefore still rendered the OLD
fused figure, directly contradicting the #56-corrected headline card sitting
above it on the SAME page — exactly the "one label, two values" defect leg
a2 exists to catch, and it caught it.

Fix: excluded the 10 known collision pe_blis from decade_grains at its
single construction point inside _write_all_sidecars (feeds BOTH the
per-program decade_series sidecar field AND years_matrix.json's /years/
grid, which reads decade_grains independently) — an honest gap ("No
year-over-year trajectory row for this line") rather than a number that
might still fuse two programs on some of its ten editions. book_diff is
nested inside the same `if pe_bli in decade_series_by_pe` guard in both
sidecar-writing loops, so it is suppressed for free.

Verified: `node -e "import('./scripts/gates/basis.mjs').then(...)"` against
the rebuilt site now returns leg a2 with 0 errors for these keys (see FULL
LOOP result below).

(2) DOSSIER CLAIMS: fact_id_derived() hashes (surface, key, metric) — a
STABLE id across rebuilds — but a #56 re-key changes WHICH VALUE that
stable id resolves to. '3010' and '3050' both have hand-generated (LLM,
2026-07-05) dossiers whose "why_it_matters" prose hardcodes dollar figures
computed under the OLD fused fct_budget_trajectory, e.g. 3010: "The
program's total FY2026 funding across both accounts is $2,620,900 thousand"
citing fact_id 21956c874a3b2de1 — same fact_id, but its recorded_value is
now 20,900 post-fix. The claim's citation still "resolves" (passes the
existing #52-era unresolvable-citation check untouched) while its own
hardcoded English sentence now contradicts the number its own footnote
chip would show if clicked.

Fix: extended _emit_dossier_sidecars with _claim_value_still_matches — for
a claim whose citation carries a numeric recorded_value, parse every
"$X,XXX thousand" figure in the claim's own text (findall, not the first
match only) and drop the claim if NONE of them are within 1% of the
citation's current value. Two false-positive patterns found and fixed
during validation before shipping:
  a) .search() (first match only) mis-flagged sentences stating TWO real,
     both-still-correct figures ("...rises from $2,424,208 thousand in
     FY2025 to $3,479,362 thousand requested for FY2026" — one sentence,
     one citation, two numbers) — 15 dossiers' accurate claims wrongly
     dropped. Fixed: findall + match-ANY, not match-the-first.
  b) "a decrease of $X thousand" claims cite a NEGATIVE recorded_value
     (-X, a real signed change fact) while English prose states the bare
     magnitude (X) — 3 more dossiers' accurate claims wrongly dropped.
     Fixed: compare the stated magnitude against BOTH the value and its
     negation.
Final, validated result (simulated directly against citations.json before
committing to a full re-export, to iterate fast): exactly 3 claims dropped
across exactly 2 dossiers — 3010 (2 claims: the FY2026 total, the FY2024
actuals) and 3050 (1 claim: the FY2026 total) — precisely the #56 keys,
zero false positives elsewhere. The pre-existing #52 unresolvable-citation
mechanism (unrelated to #56, natural corpus drift since 2026-08-08) still
independently drops 27 claims across 5 other dossiers — unchanged by this
fix, correctly left alone.

The dossier's "Correction" ScopeNote (program-dossier.tsx) previously
hardcoded #52's wording ("cited lobbying mentions that did not meet the
evidence standard") for EVERY drop reason — which would have been FALSE
for a #56 drop (nothing to do with lobbying). Extended the sidecar schema
with `dropped_reasons: {unresolvable_citation, stale_value}` so the
component can state the actual reason.

FULL LOOP after all fixes: `uv run dbt build` 134/135 (1 pre-existing
unrelated warn) / `uv run python -m govbudget export-site` clean / `uv run
pytest -q` 1,551 passed / `uv run python -m govbudget verify-phase5b1`
PASS (derived_formula_and_value=PASS specifically) / `cd site && npm run
build` succeeds, 1,993 program pages / basis.mjs leg h: PASS (h1, h2, h3
all clean, 1,993 pages resolved) / basis.mjs leg a2: 0 errors on all ten
#56 keys post-fix.

================================================================================
BACKLOG #56 — CORRECTION: THE OTHER PROGRAM DID NOT JUST STOP BEING FUSED,
IT VANISHED FROM THE PUBLISHED CORPUS (coordinator review, same day)
================================================================================

The dbt/export/gate fix above correctly stops fct_budget_trajectory from
SUMMING two accounts into one figure. What it does NOT do on its own is
represent the account that was NOT picked — dim_programs and
fct_budget_trajectory both stay pe_bli-unique, so the losing account (LPD
Flight II, Tomahawk, Naval Strike Missile, and seven more — $5.74B combined)
has no row, no trajectory, no page, and — until this correction — no
disclosure anywhere. "Corpus count unchanged (1,739 before/after)" was
reported as a good property; it is actually the tell that ten real programs
were silently dropped, not split. Backlog #49's own coverage math (index vs.
universe dollars) caught the SHRINKAGE honestly and automatically — the
percentage moved from 59.3% to 58.0% on its own — but named none of the ten
specific programs responsible for it.

FIX: `excluded` in build_programs_coverage() is now computed by GROUPING
each pe_bli's own fct_budget_lines rows by TITLE (a collision manifests as
>1 distinct title under one shared pe_bli) and diffing each title's sum
against what programs.json's trajectory actually counts for that pe_bli —
never a hardcoded criterion, so it is derived from what is ACTUALLY absent
and cannot drift out of sync with a future re-key. Two reasons, both real:
'no_detail' (#49's original case — the whole pe_bli never became a page)
and 'key_collision' (#56's — the pe_bli IS a page, one program under it
won, but a DIFFERENT, unrelated program sharing the same numeric key lost).
A new programs_excluded.json ships the FULL, untruncated list (no size
cutoff) as the completeness source a gate can check — largest_excluded
(site_meta.json, the page's own prose) stays top-5-only by design, since a
sentence cannot name hundreds of lines.

The recompute (site/scripts/gates/programs-excluded-recompute.py) reads
fct_budget_lines DIRECTLY from the duckdb warehouse — never programs.json's
or programs_excluded.json's own upstream reasoning — so this is not a
tautological "does the exporter agree with itself" check; it independently
re-derives the universe and catches BOTH shapes: a whole pe_bli absent (the
0465/0544/1250 "no_detail" lines), AND a title absent under a pe_bli that IS
present (the 3050/4217 "key_collision" lines, caught the same way for the
same reason on the SAME run — the check does not need to know in advance
which shape a given absence will be).

CORRECTED SELF-REVIEW: "Corpus count unchanged" is NOT evidence of
correctness for this defect class — it is the SYMPTOM. A program dropped
outright and a program correctly split both leave dim_programs' row count
exactly where it was; only a completeness check against the raw universe
(this leg) can tell them apart. Filed as the standing lesson for any future
task in this cluster: never report an unchanged row count as reassuring
without also checking whether every dollar the count used to represent is
still named somewhere.

BACKLOG #59 — THE RECONCILIATION DISCLOSURE NEVER REACHED THE ROLLUPS
================================================================================

SCOPE, checked first: the review's claim was "agency totals are computed
on a different basis than the rows beneath them" — NOT reproducible.
agencies.json's fy2024_total_millions equals the sum of dim_programs.
fy2024_actual_millions for every one of the 23 shipped agencies, diff
0.000 (F/A/N/OSD/MDA spot-checked, 2026-08-11). What IS live is the
review's own closing sentence: program pages already carry a
reconciliation strip disclosing the P-40-vs-TOA divergence
(ReconciliationStrip, [data-reconciliation]) and it never reached the
rollups that sum those same P-40 figures.

MEASURED (dim_programs-scoped, matching agencies.json's own
program_count and fy2024_total_millions denominator):

  org   programs  not-reconciled  P-40 total(M)   TOA total(M)   gap(M)
  A          507             100     55,456.672     59,844.067  4,387.395
  F          519              34     76,086.623     78,628.205  2,541.582
  N          387               5     40,438.644     41,143.184    704.540
  OSD        128               1     10,600.446     10,959.502    359.056

  140 programs, $7,992.573M corpus-wide (dim_programs-scoped).

SUBSTITUTION, evidenced (the task brief's own prescribed measurement
script iterates programs.json, not dim_programs): programs.json carries
2 synthesized trajectory-only rows (backlog #17 — feed events referencing
pe_blis in fct_budget_trajectory but not dim_programs) whose
fy2024_actual_millions is None. The prescribed script folds that absence
to $0 and compares it against fct_budget_lines' real TOA figure,
manufacturing a "reconciliation gap" for a program with no P-40 basis
figure to reconcile against anything. Measured 2026-08-11: pe_bli
0708083D (org A) contributes a phantom $1,000.467M; pe_bli 0603115DHA
(org DHA — which has NO dim_programs row and therefore no /agency/ page
at all) contributes a phantom $2,067.717M — $3,068.184M combined
(11,060.757 naive vs 7,992.573 dim_programs-scoped = 3,068.184, exact).
Scoping the disclosure's Python computation to dim_programs (the SAME
set agencies.json's program_count and fy2024_total_millions already use,
and what /agency/{org}/ actually renders aggregate figures from) excludes
both — the tighter, correct number, not a hardcoded shrink.

fully_reconciled CHECKED, and it does NOT mean "this program's FY24
figure differs from fy_2024_actuals": it is bool_and(reconciled) across
ALL FOUR workbook scenarios (PriorYear/CurrentYear/BudgetYearOne/
BudgetYearOneBase — dbt/models/marts/dim_programs.sql), so a program can
be fully_reconciled:false purely on a FY2025/FY2026 mismatch while its
FY2024 actuals matches the TOA figure exactly. Measured 2026-08-11:
1,397 programs are fully_reconciled:false corpus-wide, but only 142 (the
naive count) / 140 (dim_programs-scoped) actually diverge on FY2024
specifically — 1,271 of the 1,397 have NO FY2024 divergence at all. Using
the badge's own flag for this note would have overstated what the FY2024
column excludes by roughly 10x. The disclosure's count is computed
independently in Python (export_site.py, agencies.json's
fy2024_not_reconciled_count) directly from the same TOA-vs-P40 comparison
Step 1 measured, never from fully_reconciled.

FIX: export_site.py mints a per-agency P-1/R-1 TOA-basis derived citation
(fact_id_derived("agency", org, "fy2024_toa_actuals_millions"),
inputs=[] + a literal runnable query_body — a same-shape fact-id input
list as the existing fy2024_total_millions citation would ~double every
/agency/{org}/ page's embedded citation payload for no reader benefit,
and /agency/F/ has only ~10KB of gzip headroom under its
PAGE_WEIGHT_BUDGET ceiling) alongside the existing P-40-basis
fy2024_total_millions, plus the per-agency non-reconciled program count.
/agency/{org}/'s page.tsx renders a ScopeNote (data-note-kind="scope")
reusing reconciliation-strip.tsx's own header line ("Two official
figures, one label") and mechanism sentence almost verbatim rather than
inventing a second vocabulary, with both totals as real <Cite> figures
(basis="toa"/"jbook-detail", no data-entity — this is an agency
aggregate, not a per-program figure, so leg (e)'s FIGURE-form cross-page
join correctly never sees it) and the delta computed via
reproducibleDifference (site/src/lib/derivation.ts), the same
arithmetic-closes-at-display-precision helper ReconciliationStrip already
uses — never a second delta-formatting idiom. Rendered only when
fy2024_not_reconciled_count > 0 (4 of 23 agencies: A, F, N, OSD).

prescribed floor, "20 agency pages," read as "20 pages that RENDER the

agency pages have any FY2024 P-40-vs-TOA divergence at all; a 5th
apparent org, DHA, is the phantom explained above and correctly ships no
/agency/ page). Substituted the SAME number (20) against "agency pages
RESOLVED" (built HTML found and joined to an agencies.json sidecar row)
instead — leg h3's own precedent ("100 program pages resolve a sidecar",
not "100 pages exhibit the fused-card defect") for exactly this reason:
leg g3 uses "resolved = matched the condition" because FY2026
reconciliation-split affects ≥100 programs, a naturally large true-
positive count; #59's condition is rare by construction (4 of 23), so
h3's convention — corpus-coverage floor, not condition-count floor — is
the one that applies. i1/i2 (completeness + no-spurious-render) are what
actually check the defect, unconditionally, on every resolved page; i3
only proves the leg walked real pages instead of a token handful. 23
agency pages ship, so 20-of-23 resolved (~87%) is still a real,
non-trivial requirement — never loosened, retargeted at what the site's
own data can support.
